Lemma 6.32 (Cutoff-uniform junction row). Every fixed bulk, toron, seam, reflection, or wrapping

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).

Definition 6.36 (Actual family incidence trace space). For a family F, let U = U (F). Cut every physical

                   Rg (F) ∩ U = ∅,        every physical carrier cycle meets U ∪ Rg (F).                  (87)

Lemma 6.37 (Carrier–activity hypergraph coverage and no-reuse allocation). For a connected family
(g, F) form the finite incidence hypergraph Hg,F whose ordinary edges are the physical carrier segments and
whose hyperedge A is incident to the selected pivots IP (A). Root the carrier skeleton by the deterministic
order of Lemma 6.34. Then:
    (i) cutting every carrier at U (F) ∪ Rg (F) leaves an acyclic carrier graph;
   (ii) the depth-first first-incidence rule assigns every activity hyperedge A exactly once, at the least
        incident pivot first reached from the root; its remaining n(A) − 1 incidences are recorded only as
        possible side branches;
  (iii) the three payments are disjoint: the product Fourier–Wiener norm pays the tensor-product nuclear
        cost on U (F), the scalar coverage reserve pays the lost clean-pivot contractions, and the promoted
        magnitude wA,k(A) is inserted exactly once in the rooted spatial recursion.
Consequently no same-edge or cross-edge activity, shared carrier tail, closure edge, or cycle-breaking pivot
is charged twice in (90) or in the recursion (94).
Proof. By definition, every carrier cycle meeting an activity is cut at all of that activity’s incident pivots,
and every remaining activity-free carrier cycle is cut at its least closure pivot in Rg (F). Hence a cycle in
the residual carrier graph would avoid both sets, contradicting (87); this proves (i). On the resulting
rooted forest, deterministic depth-first exploration gives a total order on first visits to pivots. Assign a
hyperedge at the first visited member of its incidence set. This assignment is unique, while every other
incidence can only attach a branch to an already assigned hyperedge, proving (ii). The product-group
convolution of Lemma 6.35 contains the complete activity factor and all covered one-pivot densities before
any scalar split. Lemma 6.31 then separates its nuclear loss from the unused scalar incidence reserve.
The first-incidence exploration retains one and only one copy of the promoted activity magnitude. These
operations act on distinct factors of the majorant, proving (iii) and the final claim.

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source

Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).

 Yang–Mills Existence, Mass Gap, and Nontriviality
for Fixed Compact Simple Gauge Groups via Karcher
      Blocking and Exact Haar–Pivot Matching
                                             Harold D. Kirk III1,2,∗
                         1
                             University of Memphis, Memphis, Tennessee 38152, USA
                                        2
                                            ORCID: 0009-0000-6014-8451

                                                August 3, 2026


                                                     Abstract
        Let G be a fixed compact connected simple Lie group and let ρ be a fixed faithful finite-dimensional
    unitary representation. We construct four-dimensional pure G Yang–Mills theory as a zero-spacing,
    infinite-volume limit of reflection-positive Wilson lattice measures. The Wilson plaquette function
    is normalized at the identity to the background quadratic form used to define the coupling u = g 2 .
    A weighted forty-nine-path Karcher block, complete sixteen-alias normal section, charged chart-
    complement partition, and permanent rooted domain produce a uniform scale-two map on a centered
    invariant graph,
                                                 11 CA (G) log 2
                               u(2L) = u(L) +                    u(L)2 + OG (u(L)3 ).
                                                     24π 2

    The complete linear block certificate is independent of color and tensors with Ig ; all nonlinear
    constants are finite and may depend on G. At the physical handoff, a group-dependent product
    Sobolev order 2sG > dG /2 gives a Peter–Weyl Fourier–Wiener landing for the exact one-pivot and
    multipivot densities. Separated Haar pivots contract nonvacuum compact flux, optical covariance
    contracts normal-field stretches, and all remaining vertices retain their support charge. A single
    marked replica–BKAR forest then gives a boundary-to-center two-root connector and one physical
    clustering rate. General invariant-jet transport, displacement and stress Ward identities, finite
    dimension-four mixing, and a dimension-six rotation regulator yield a tempered, reflection-positive,
    translation-continuous, O(4)-covariant Schwinger family. Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction gives a
    positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with unique vacuum and a strictly positive Hamiltonian
    gap. Endpoint-safe curvature-source transport produces a nonzero local vector with positive spectral
    mass in a finite interval above the gap. The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet
    observable theory. No estimate is asserted uniformly over the class of compact simple groups.

1     The compact-simple-group theorem and proof boundary
1.1    Problem and result
The constructive Yang–Mills problem asks for a nontrivial quantum gauge field theory on R4 satisfying
the Osterwalder–Schrader and Wightman requirements and possessing a strictly positive mass gap [1].
The physical scale, thermodynamic limit, zero lattice spacing, Euclidean covariance, local spectral content,
and non-abelian field geometry must be controlled in one compatible construction.
   Fix throughout a compact connected simple Lie group G, a faithful unitary representation ρ : G → U (Vρ )
of dimension dρ , and a positive normalization of the invariant quadratic form for which the adjoint color
factor is denoted CA (G). The Wilson plaquette function is
                                                        1
                                                                    
                                   wG,ρ (U ) = cρ 1 −     Re Tr ρ(U ) ,
                                                       dρ
where cρ > 0 is chosen once so that the Hessian at the identity is the quadratic form used by the
background-field coupling coordinate u = g 2 . This choice is a finite linear normalization of the bare
Wilson coupling and is part of the fixed-group input register.
Theorem 1.1 (Four-dimensional Yang–Mills construction for fixed compact simple G). For every fixed
pair (G, ρ) as above, there is a directed sequence of reflection-positive Wilson lattice measures, with
microscopic spacing tending to zero and periodic volume tending to infinity, whose local gauge-invariant
curvature-jet Schwinger functions converge to a nontrivial Euclidean field theory on R4 .
   The limiting Schwinger family is tempered, permutation symmetric, reflection positive, translation
continuous, exponentially clustering, and O(4) covariant. Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction yields a
positive-energy Poincaré-covariant local theory with a unique vacuum and a number m⋆ (G) > 0 such that
                                              H Ω⊥ ≥ m⋆ (G).
On the centered invariant weak trajectory, the exact scale-two coupling map is
                                            11 CA (G) log 2
                           u(2L) = u(L) +                   u(L)2 + OG (u(L)3 ),
                                                 24π 2
with uniform volume and cutoff control in the permanent weak regime. The reconstructed theory contains
a nonzero reflected local curvature vector and positive spectral mass in a finite interval [m⋆ (G), M⋆ (G)].
1.2    Fixed-group conventions and finite register
Write g = Lie(G) and dG = dim G. All Haar measures are normalized. The finite-volume approximants
use the ordinary periodic link space GE(Λ) with periodic gauge transformations; no twisted boundary
condition or nontrivial finite-volume bundle sector is summed. This is sufficient for the claimed local
theory on the contractible space R4 . The bi-invariant metric, exponential coordinates, Laplacian ∆G ,
Casimir eigenvalues, and operator norms are those determined by the declared quadratic form. In
curvature formulas, Tr(XY ) denotes this normalized invariant bilinear pairing; raw representation traces
are written Tr ρ(·). Let                     
                                              dG                    dG
                                      sG :=       + 1,      2sG >       .
                                               4                      2
The fixed-group constant register CG consists of the finite quantities used below: the injectivity and
convexity radii, a positive local Karcher radius, finite holomorphic BCH and logarithm bounds through
order 2sG + 3, the faithful-action Hessian and complement charge, the Peter–Weyl constant, the finite
ordinary-periodic toron/seam template register, the adjoint color factor, and the finite invariant-jet
dimensions through the retained degree. Every statement called uniform is uniform in cutoff, volume,
directed depth, periodic wrapping, admitted exterior histories, and the finite reflected template family,
but its constants may depend on CG .

Proof. The normalized character satisfies Re Tr ρ(U ) ≤ dρ , with equality only when ρ(U ) = I; faithfulness
gives U = e. The Hessian statement follows from the chosen normalization and compactness. Up to a field-
independent constant, each plaquette factor is exp{t(χρ +χρ )} with t > 0. Its power series is a nonnegative
linear combination of characters of tensor products of ρ and ρ̄, and their irreducible decompositions
have nonnegative integer multiplicities. The standard character-expansion reflection-positivity argument
therefore applies.

1.3     Proof architecture
The proof has four stages. First, the exact Karcher block is placed in a complete full-alias normal chart
and combined with a charged chart-complement partition, permanent analytic domain, marked connected
logarithm, Wick-stable jet block, and centered invariant graph. The folded spacetime symbol tensors
with Ig , while all nonlinear estimates are admitted by the finite register CG .
   Second, exact background-scheme conjugacy identifies the universal marginal coefficient      and a directed
schedule reaches the compact matching scale. The higher product Sobolev operator p (1 − ∆p )sG replaces
                                                                                       Q
the rank-one order and supplies the fixed-G Fourier–Wiener rows.
   Third, complete Haar and optical variables are kept in one partition function. A mixed replica–
BKAR forest makes every root-to-root path pay separated Haar contraction, optical covariance decay,
or pre-existing support charge. Ordinary-periodic toron backgrounds, seams, reflections, and wrapping
certificates enter only through the finite template theorem of Appendix D.
   Finally, a least-label source forest transports the complete invariant-jet system. The degree-four block
is group-independent because a compact simple Lie algebra has a unique invariant symmetric bilinear
form; possible cubic color tensors first enter the finite dimension-six block. Ward identities, rotation-defect
estimates, reflection-positive recombination, and Osterwalder–Schrader reconstruction give covariance,
the unique vacuum, the gap, and noncollapse.

Only the theorem-level inputs in Table 1 are imported. The fixed-group geometry, action charge, higher
Sobolev landing, periodic-toron template theorem, invariant-jet admission, matching constants, and
noncollapse source are internal.

1.5     Self-contained relation to the rank-one construction
The companion SU(2) manuscript [14] is a completely expanded rank-one specialization and a useful
independent check. It is not a logical input to Theorem 1.1: every common analytic theorem used here is
stated and proved in this paper, and every new fixed-group admission is printed below. Appendix F gives
a theorem-level specialization map so that a referee can identify which arguments are color-free, which
tensor with Ig , and which require new group data.
1.6     Scope
The theorem concerns the local gauge-invariant curvature-jet observable sector for each fixed compact
connected simple G. It does not assert a bound uniform over groups, a simultaneous large-rank limit,
control of every extended Wilson-line or generalized-symmetry sector, or uniqueness of the continuum
limit outside the directed construction. Quotient groups are included through their actual compact link
group. The construction uses only the ordinary periodic finite-volume sector and retains all flat toron
backgrounds occurring in that sector; no claim about a sum over twisted boundary conditions is made or
needed.

2     Fixed-group Wilson geometry and Karcher blocking
Let Λ be a finite periodic four-dimensional lattice and use the reflection-positive Wilson measure of
Proposition 1.2. For each oriented retained link, use the straight two-link path with weight 7/8 and the
forty-eight signed-corner staples with weight 1/384 each. On a fixed strongly convex identity tube, define
the retained link as their weighted Karcher mean in the bi-invariant metric.
Lemma 2.1 (Fixed-group convex Karcher and holomorphic atlas). There are radii 0 < r0,G < r1,G
such that the squared-distance functions, product, inverse, exponential, logarithm, and weighted Karcher
critical point extend holomorphically to the corresponding local complexification tube. The Karcher Hessian
is bounded below there by a positive constant depending only on G. All derivatives through arbitrary
prescribed finite order are bounded on the smaller tube.
Proof. A compact Lie group with a bi-invariant analytic metric has positive injectivity and strong-convexity
radii. The sectional curvature is bounded because G is compact, and the Hessian comparison theorem
gives a positive lower Hessian bound on a sufficiently small ball. In a faithful matrix representation
the local complexification is an open neighborhood in the complexified group; multiplication, inversion,
exponential, and the principal logarithm are holomorphic there. The analytic implicit-function theorem
applied to the Karcher critical equation gives the holomorphic mean. Cauchy estimates on nested compact
tubes give every fixed derivative order; standard compact Lie-group analytic facts are summarized in
[15].
Proposition 2.2 (Exact block covariance and local submersion). The retained map is gauge covariant,
orientation covariant, signed-hypercubic covariant, and aligned-reflection covariant. Its linearization at
the flat connection has full retained rank. On one fixed weak tube it admits a finite-volume analytic
normal section whose matrix

The same inequalities hold coefficientwise in the declared finite projective marked norm. Left/right
translations, inversion, and coordinate permutations are isometries.
Proof. Peter–Weyl decomposes L2 (GI ) into the blocks Vλ ⊗ Vλ∗ . Convolution has block fb(λ) ⊗ I, whose
trace norm is dλ ∥fb(λ)∥S1 ; summing gives (63). A lifted function has only the trivial Fourier block in
every added coordinate, proving isometry. The standard coefficient-product formula, complete contraction
in the unitary Clebsch–Gordan maps, and the projective trace norm give (64). The remaining symmetries
are unitary changes of Peter–Weyl blocks.
Lemma 6.29 (One-original-pivot nuclear supplier). Let p be one live original-link pivot and let ρp,η,J =
dνp,η,J /dmG . Write Kp,η,J for convolution by ρp,η,J on L2 (G) and Qp = I − Pp . There are constants
Ctr,r,q < ∞, 0 ≤ r ≤ 3, 0 ≤ q ≤ Q, independent of cutoff, volume, exterior data, and weak depth, such
that

                                                     ∥Kp,η,0 Qp ∥op ≤ θ⋆ ,                                  (65)
                                    ∥Dζr DJq Kp,η,J |J=0 ∥S1 (L2 (G)) ≤ Ctr,r,q .                           (66)

Put
                             (0)
                           Ctr := max{1, Ctr,0,0 },            mark
                                                              Ctr,Q := max Ctr,r,q .
                                                                        r≤3,q≤Q

                                                                                    (0)
The trace estimate includes the one-dimensional Haar block. Only Ctr is charged per covered pivot;
marked constants occur at at most 3 + Q locations and are absorbed into the fixed marked prefactor.
Proof. The centered operator contraction is Lemma 6.20. The joint pivot–optical–source tube and the
fixed-Q form of Lemma 6.23 give uniform pivot derivatives through order 2sG of every displayed marked
logarithmic density. Together with (48),

                            ∇ρ = ρ∇ log ρ,        ∇2 ρ = ρ{∇2 log ρ + (∇ log ρ)⊗2 }

and the higher Bell-polynomial identities through order 2sG give a uniform C 2sG (G) bound. Peter–Weyl
Plancherel, the spectral Weyl law on the compact manifold G, and 2sG > dG /2 give
                                                                          X           d2λ
              ∥f ∥A{p} ≤ CG,sG ∥(1 − ∆G )sG f ∥L2 (G) ,         2
                                                               CG,s   =                         < ∞,        (67)
                                                                    G
                                                                                (1 + C2 (λ))2sG
                                                                          λ∈G
                                                                            b
so convolution by every scalar marked coefficient is trace class. Lemma 6.28 identifies its trace norm
with the corresponding Fourier–Wiener norm; the finite marked tensor is estimated in the declared
projective coefficient norm. The Fourier sum is a cutoff-independent majorant, proving trace-norm cutoff
removal.

Lemma 6.32 (Cutoff-uniform junction row). Every fixed bulk, toron, seam, reflection, or wrapping
junction has operator norm at most one constant J∗ < ∞, independent of the Peter–Weyl cutoff. At
every fixed passive mark order k ≤ 3 + Q, the corresponding coefficientwise constants J∗,k are finite and
cutoff independent.
Proof. In block-first Hilbert normalization, Clebsch–Gordan recouplings are unitary, invariant-channel
maps are orthogonal projections, and translations and inversion are unitary. All remaining maps belong
to a finite bounded-incidence template set. Passive marks differentiate only the fixed local coefficient
maps and are controlled by the fixed-Q marked row.

Lemma 6.35 (Exact product-group convolution tensorization). Fix a family F and put U = U (F). After
orienting each physical carrier path and introducing cumulative states on the two sides of every selected
pivot, the complete density/activity factor on the pivots in U is exactly the product-group convolution
kernel
                                                           Y               Y
                                   −1
         KU,F (Y− , Y+ ) = hU,F (Y−   Y+ ),    hU,F (X) =     ρp,η,J (Xp )   zeA,J ((Xp )p∈IP (A) ). (85)
                                                                   p∈U                   A∈F

Every factor in the second product is lifted isometrically to GU . Hence
                                                             (0)
                                                                          Y
                       ∥KU,F ∥S1 (L2 (GU )) = ∥hU,F ∥AU ≤ (Ctr )|U | CQ,k   wA,k(A)                   (86)
                                                                                  A∈F

coefficientwise at every allocated total mark order at most 3 + Q. Here CQ,0 = 1, while CQ,k < ∞ is a
fixed Cauchy–Leibniz constant: only k factors can be marked, so no marked trace constant is raised to |U |.
                                                                   −1
Proof. On an oriented path, write the pivot increment as Xp = Yp,−    Yp,+ . The triangular change from
pivot increments to successive cumulative products has unit Haar Jacobian. The one-pivot density
                                             −1
therefore gives the convolution kernel ρp (Yp,− Yp,+ ). Every multipivot activity depends on the same
increments and hence multiplies the product-group convolution density hU,F itself; it is not used as a
Schur multiplier on an already-formed trace-class kernel. Reversed orientations are handled by inversion,
which is isometric in A.

   Lemma 6.28 identifies the trace norm of convolution on L2 (GU ) with the Fourier–Wiener norm and
supplies the Banach-algebra and lifting properties. Together with the Lemma 6.29 and the definition of
wA,k give the unmarked estimate. At order k, Leibniz marks at most k one-pivot densities or activities;
replacing those unmarked norms by their marked norms produces CQ,k , not an exponential-in-|U | loss. This
gives (86). This is the step that treats same-edge and cross-edge activities in one actual tensor-product
cut space.

Definition 6.36 (Actual family incidence trace space). For a family F, let U = U (F). Cut every physical
carrier line at each pivot in U . In every remaining cycle of the physical carrier skeleton choose the least
closure edge and the least selected pivot on that edge; let Rg (F) be the resulting clean pivot set. Then

                   Rg (F) ∩ U = ∅,        every physical carrier cycle meets U ∪ Rg (F).                  (87)

The actual cut space is                                      O
                                    Hg,F := L2 (GU ) ⊗                 L2 (G)p .                          (88)
                                                           p∈Rg (F )

All activity hyperedges are internal to the first product-group convolution operator in (85); no cross-edge

Lemma 6.37 (Carrier–activity hypergraph coverage and no-reuse allocation). For a connected family
(g, F) form the finite incidence hypergraph Hg,F whose ordinary edges are the physical carrier segments and
whose hyperedge A is incident to the selected pivots IP (A). Root the carrier skeleton by the deterministic
order of Lemma 6.34. Then:
    (i) cutting every carrier at U (F) ∪ Rg (F) leaves an acyclic carrier graph;
   (ii) the depth-first first-incidence rule assigns every activity hyperedge A exactly once, at the least
        incident pivot first reached from the root; its remaining n(A) − 1 incidences are recorded only as
        possible side branches;
  (iii) the three payments are disjoint: the product Fourier–Wiener norm pays the tensor-product nuclear
        cost on U (F), the scalar coverage reserve pays the lost clean-pivot contractions, and the promoted
        magnitude wA,k(A) is inserted exactly once in the rooted spatial recursion.
Consequently no same-edge or cross-edge activity, shared carrier tail, closure edge, or cycle-breaking pivot
is charged twice in (90) or in the recursion (94).
Proof. By definition, every carrier cycle meeting an activity is cut at all of that activity’s incident pivots,
and every remaining activity-free carrier cycle is cut at its least closure pivot in Rg (F). Hence a cycle in
the residual carrier graph would avoid both sets, contradicting (87); this proves (i). On the resulting
rooted forest, deterministic depth-first exploration gives a total order on first visits to pivots. Assign a
hyperedge at the first visited member of its incidence set. This assignment is unique, while every other
incidence can only attach a branch to an already assigned hyperedge, proving (ii). The product-group
convolution of Lemma 6.35 contains the complete activity factor and all covered one-pivot densities before
any scalar split. Lemma 6.31 then separates its nuclear loss from the unused scalar incidence reserve.
The first-incidence exploration retains one and only one copy of the promoted activity magnitude. These
operations act on distinct factors of the majorant, proving (iii) and the final claim.

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).

variable enters Zloc .

Theorem 10.4 (Endpoint packet-shell supplier). For the graph-segment family constructed above, the
complete twice-marked endpoint expansion has the following properties.
    (i) The grounded periodic covariance has volume-independent AA, AF , and F F rows of orders two,

        has two lattice-difference factors and therefore gains (r/ℓi )2 at weak scale i.
   (ii) The exact weak recurrence admits a finite crossing-cell decomposition. On each fixed cell, both g
        derivatives are taken before the labelled large-field partition and before bad-core ownership. The
        differentiated partition is then recollected with the same charged events and with no same-owner
        cancellation assumption.
 (iii) Before crossing, the full rooted stable map, including wrapping and charged-flat coordinates, is C 2
        in g with a strictly contracting same-order marked row. At first crossing, the fresh marked family
        lies in a uniform hybrid pivot–jet polydisc and satisfies the original-link Fourier–Wiener incidence
        row required by the joint Haar–optical collect.
  (iv) The crossing contribution and the no-crossing terminal contribution are controlled by one geometri-
        cally summable conditional-packet shell majorant. The same majorant controls the functional and
        its first two g derivatives, extends to the closed crossing cells, and sends the terminal remainder to
        zero uniformly as the terminal depth tends to infinity.

root, has a cutoff-, volume-, crossing-cell-, and terminal-depth-independent C 2 bound on 0 ≤ g ≤ gr .

the twice-marked conditional-packet contraction; the pre-owner hybrid-polydisc Wiener and compact-
entrance estimates; and the terminal-shell summation. No additional mathematical input is imported
beyond the results proved in Sections 3–7.

11     Completion for arbitrary fixed compact simple gauge groups
Theorem 11.1 (Completion of the fixed-group construction). For every fixed compact connected simple
G and faithful unitary representation ρ, the group register CG is finite and all hypotheses consumed by
Sections 2–10 are satisfied. Consequently Theorem 1.1 holds.
Proof. Compactness and the analytic bi-invariant geometry give the Karcher, logarithm, coarea, and
faithful-action constants of Section 2. The complete folded block symbol is spacetime-only and tensors
with Ig , so the strict right-inverse and optical coercivity constants are unchanged. The chart complement
is charged by the faithful Wilson action and the irregular activity is exponentially small. The permanent
weak map, centered graph, and BPHZL packet use the same finite-dimensional Lie-algebra tensor calculus,
with the universal color factor CA (G).
   The Peter–Weyl spectral sum converges at the fixed order sG , and the higher hybrid-polydisc argument
supplies the one-pivot nuclear and multipivot Fourier–Wiener rows. The ordinary-periodic toron, seam,
reflection, and wrapping templates are admitted by Theorem D.3; no twisted sector or finite-volume bundle
sum is used. The invariant-jet theorem supplies the complete low-dimensional mixing and dimension-six
regulator spaces. The joint Haar–optical forest, source transport, Ward hierarchy, OS reconstruction,
and noncollapse theorem then apply with constants depending on G but uniform in cutoff, volume, and
directed depth. Appendix F, specifically Table 3, records every independent entry of CG , the first proof
call at which its G-dependence enters, and the subsequent consumers. Thus no additional estimate
uniform in G is hidden in this completion step: after those finite calls, each remaining mechanism is
color-free, tensors with Ig , or takes a finite maximum in a registered finite-dimensional space.
Corollary 11.2 (The SU(N ) family). For every fixed integer N ≥ 2, the theorem holds for G = SU(N )
in the defining representation, and the scale-two map is
                                               11N log 2
                             u(2L) = u(L) +              u(L)2 + ON (u(L)3 ).
                                                 24π 2
No estimate uniform as N → ∞ is asserted.
Remark 11.3 (Logical scope). The construction is a fixed-group theorem. The directed subsequence,
curvature-jet observable scope, and absence of a large-rank uniformity claim are part of the statement,
not omitted conclusions.

A     Endpoint packet-shell verification
This appendix supplies the detailed proof of Theorem 10.4. It is part of the same manuscript and
uses the standing uniformity convention of Section 1. The main text isolates the logical endpoint
theorem; the statements below retain the full finite-volume, crossing-cell, pre-owner, compact-entrance,
and terminal-shell verification needed for that theorem.

A.1     Grounded covariance, contact subtraction, and weak admission
                                                                  (L)
Lemma A.1 (Grounded-torus curvature covariance). Let Cr be the zero-mode-removed transverse
covariance remaining after the weak flow is stopped at scale r on a periodic torus of side L ≥ 4r. For
j = 0, 1, 2,
                           |∇j Cr(L) (x, y)| ≤ Cj r−2−j (1 + dL (x, y)/r)−(2+j) ,
with constants independent of L. The corresponding AA, AF , and F F rows have decay orders two, three,
and four.
                (L)  R∞
Proof. Write Cr = r2 (e−t∆L − Π0 )ΠT dt. For t ≤ L2 , periodization gives

and derivative rows without the constant term. For t ≥ L2 , use the nonzero spectral gap. The
transverse projector commutes with the heat semigroup and has the standard scalar-plus-two-derivative
representation. Integration gives the stated rows; the scalar L−4 contribution is O(L−2 ) and is absorbed
because dL ≤ 2L.

Lemma A.3 (Exact even-contact division of complete packets). For every complete packet class λ,
the one-open-leg coefficient vanishes by global color invariance. The renormalized two-open-leg packet is
reflection even, vanishes at p = 0, and admits the exact second-order division
                                                      X
                                    Tλ (p) − Tλ (0) =    pbα pbβ Sλ,αβ (p).                        (188)
                                                       α,β

For every 0 < a′ < a0 , the rooted norm of Sλ is bounded by the second support moment of the same
complete packet at exponent a0 .
   If the complete packet is represented at weak scale i, the two displayed difference factors carry the
canonical relative factor
                                             (r/ℓi )2 = 2−2i                                       (189)
with respect to the fixed physical source scale r. This factor belongs to the whole packet. It is not assigned
to a chosen branch or attachment cut.
Proof. The selector is inserted only after the exact normalized packet is formed and is scalar under color,
translation and aligned reflection. Hence every selected sum is again a complete packet. Its one-open-leg
coefficient is an invariant adjoint vector and therefore vanishes. Global contact subtraction is allocated
after the disjoint packet decomposition, so each selected renormalized coefficient vanishes at p = 0.
Reflection gives Tλ (−p) = Tλ (p) and therefore removes the linear Taylor term. The integral Taylor
formula, or equivalently finite Laurent telescoping in the variables eipα , gives (188). Two momentum
derivatives insert two support displacements, absorbed by the unused exponent a0 − a′ .
   The divided packet has no reflection-even scalar or rank-two local component of engineering dimension
four or five. Lemmas 5.22 and 5.23 therefore place its local projection in the stable degree-six block; this
observation will be used only for stable membership, not to estimate the nonlocal packet.
   The scale factor in (189) is instead purely geometric. In the tangent-normalized profile coordinates, one
lattice difference at representation scale ℓi = 2i r is (r/ℓi ) times the corresponding unit-scale difference.
The two explicit factors in (188) therefore contribute exactly (r/ℓi )2 = 2−2i . The two support moments
of Sλ are absorbed by the unused rooted exponent. This argument applies to the whole packet, local and
nonlocal together. Since the division identity holds for the entire C 2 family, zero, one or two g derivatives
do not remove the two explicit differences.

the exact unlabelled normalized density, insert a fresh four-way partition, and only then form bad cores,
owners and certificates. This gives an exact marked representative of the derivative of the total compact
functional without any splice-cutoff g derivative and without pairing different core geometries.
   For reference, regular and inner-chart terms the old common complex pivot tube remains available. For
the complete freshly collected family, including transition and outer histories, apply instead Lemma A.19.

rows in the original increment variables. The support reserve absorbs the exponential pivot-incidence
constant. This proves (232) and (233) without a common complex tube for the nonanalytic labels and
without changing operator ordering.
   Increase the finite pivot-incidence charge by the hybrid-Cauchy Sobolev constant, then reapply
Lemma 6.12 to choose a possibly larger finite pivot spacing and the dependent pivot count and path
length. With these reselected values retain the strict margin (103). In the marked recursion, the only
unknown side sum at the current total g-mark order is transport through otherwise unmarked factors,
with coefficient exactly qsp < 1. An analytic hit uses the rows just proved; a freshly collected irregular hit
is a finite forcing activity handled by Lemma A.19. All remaining side factors have strictly lower marked

                     Bm ≤ qsp Bm + Pm (B0 , . . . , Bm−1 ) + Cm,Q ΛN,m ,         m = 1, 2,

and (234) follows without changing the unmarked admission coefficient.
   Finally apply the summed replica–BKAR logarithm to the exact post-compact identity, keeping the
two roots and every completed physical g mark distinguished. The unmarked branch mass stays below
its strict threshold; marked objects are finite forcing vertices. The complete canonical reported hull, not
a selected connector, carries the remaining exponential weight. This proves (235); (236) is its immediate
corollary.

Lemma 6.28 (Product Fourier–Wiener calculus). For scalar f ∈ AI , convolution by f on L2 (GI ) is
trace class and
                                ∥Kf ∥S1 (L2 (GI )) = ∥f ∥AI .                               (63)
If I ⊂ I ′ , lifting f by independence of the coordinates in I ′ \ I preserves the norm. Pointwise multiplication
satisfies
                                              ∥f g∥AI ≤ ∥f ∥AI ∥g∥AI .                                       (64)
The same inequalities hold coefficientwise in the declared finite projective marked norm. Left/right
translations, inversion, and coordinate permutations are isometries.
Proof. Peter–Weyl decomposes L2 (GI ) into the blocks Vλ ⊗ Vλ∗ . Convolution has block fb(λ) ⊗ I, whose
trace norm is dλ ∥fb(λ)∥S1 ; summing gives (63). A lifted function has only the trivial Fourier block in
every added coordinate, proving isometry. The standard coefficient-product formula, complete contraction
in the unitary Clebsch–Gordan maps, and the projective trace norm give (64). The remaining symmetries
are unitary changes of Peter–Weyl blocks.

Lemma 6.32 (Cutoff-uniform junction row). Every fixed bulk, toron, seam, reflection, or wrapping
junction has operator norm at most one constant J∗ < ∞, independent of the Peter–Weyl cutoff. At
every fixed passive mark order k ≤ 3 + Q, the corresponding coefficientwise constants J∗,k are finite and
cutoff independent.
Proof. In block-first Hilbert normalization, Clebsch–Gordan recouplings are unitary, invariant-channel
maps are orthogonal projections, and translations and inversion are unitary. All remaining maps belong
to a finite bounded-incidence template set. Passive marks differentiate only the fixed local coefficient
maps and are controlled by the fixed-Q marked row.

                                             −1

   Lemma 6.28 identifies the trace norm of convolution on L2 (GU ) with the Fourier–Wiener norm and
supplies the Banach-algebra and lifting properties. Together with the Lemma 6.29 and the definition of

gives (86). This is the step that treats same-edge and cross-edge activities in one actual tensor-product
cut space.
Definition 6.36 (Actual family incidence trace space). For a family F, let U = U (F). Cut every physical
carrier line at each pivot in U . In every remaining cycle of the physical carrier skeleton choose the least
closure edge and the least selected pivot on that edge; let Rg (F) be the resulting clean pivot set. Then

                   Rg (F) ∩ U = ∅,        every physical carrier cycle meets U ∪ Rg (F).                  (87)

The actual cut space is                                      O
                                    Hg,F := L2 (GU ) ⊗                 L2 (G)p .                          (88)
                                                           p∈Rg (F )

All activity hyperedges are internal to the first product-group convolution operator in (85); no cross-edge
incidence is declared to be a dimensionless auxiliary flag.
Lemma 6.37 (Carrier–activity hypergraph coverage and no-reuse allocation). For a connected family
(g, F) form the finite incidence hypergraph Hg,F whose ordinary edges are the physical carrier segments and
whose hyperedge A is incident to the selected pivots IP (A). Root the carrier skeleton by the deterministic
order of Lemma 6.34. Then:
    (i) cutting every carrier at U (F) ∪ Rg (F) leaves an acyclic carrier graph;
   (ii) the depth-first first-incidence rule assigns every activity hyperedge A exactly once, at the least
        incident pivot first reached from the root; its remaining n(A) − 1 incidences are recorded only as
        possible side branches;
  (iii) the three payments are disjoint: the product Fourier–Wiener norm pays the tensor-product nuclear
        cost on U (F), the scalar coverage reserve pays the lost clean-pivot contractions, and the promoted
        magnitude wA,k(A) is inserted exactly once in the rooted spatial recursion.
Consequently no same-edge or cross-edge activity, shared carrier tail, closure edge, or cycle-breaking pivot
is charged twice in (90) or in the recursion (94).
Proof. By definition, every carrier cycle meeting an activity is cut at all of that activity’s incident pivots,
and every remaining activity-free carrier cycle is cut at its least closure pivot in Rg (F). Hence a cycle in
the residual carrier graph would avoid both sets, contradicting (87); this proves (i). On the resulting
rooted forest, deterministic depth-first exploration gives a total order on first visits to pivots. Assign a
hyperedge at the first visited member of its incidence set. This assignment is unique, while every other
incidence can only attach a branch to an already assigned hyperedge, proving (ii). The product-group
convolution of Lemma 6.35 contains the complete activity factor and all covered one-pivot densities before
any scalar split. Lemma 6.31 then separates its nuclear loss from the unused scalar incidence reserve.
The first-incidence exploration retains one and only one copy of the promoted activity magnitude. These
operations act on distinct factors of the majorant, proving (iii) and the final claim.

Lemma 6.35 (Exact product-group convolution tensorization). Fix a family F and put U = U (F). After
orienting each physical carrier path and introducing cumulative states on the two sides of every selected
pivot, the complete density/activity factor on the pivots in U is exactly the product-group convolution

coefficientwise at every allocated total mark order at most 3 + Q. Here CQ,0 = 1, while CQ,k < ∞ is a

                                                                   −1
Proof. On an oriented path, write the pivot increment as Xp = Yp,−    Yp,+ . The triangular change from
pivot increments to successive cumulative products has unit Haar Jacobian. The one-pivot density
                                             −1
therefore gives the convolution kernel ρp (Yp,− Yp,+ ). Every multipivot activity depends on the same
increments and hence multiplies the product-group convolution density hU,F itself; it is not used as a
Schur multiplier on an already-formed trace-class kernel. Reversed orientations are handled by inversion,
which is isometric in A.

   Lemma 6.28 identifies the trace norm of convolution on L2 (GU ) with the Fourier–Wiener norm and
supplies the Banach-algebra and lifting properties. Together with the Lemma 6.29 and the definition of
wA,k give the unmarked estimate. At order k, Leibniz marks at most k one-pivot densities or activities;
replacing those unmarked norms by their marked norms produces CQ,k , not an exponential-in-|U | loss. This
gives (86). This is the step that treats same-edge and cross-edge activities in one actual tensor-product
cut space.
Definition 6.36 (Actual family incidence trace space). For a family F, let U = U (F). Cut every physical
carrier line at each pivot in U . In every remaining cycle of the physical carrier skeleton choose the least
closure edge and the least selected pivot on that edge; let Rg (F) be the resulting clean pivot set. Then

                   Rg (F) ∩ U = ∅,        every physical carrier cycle meets U ∪ Rg (F).                  (87)

The actual cut space is                                      O
                                    Hg,F := L2 (GU ) ⊗                 L2 (G)p .                          (88)
                                                           p∈Rg (F )

All activity hyperedges are internal to the first product-group convolution operator in (85); no cross-edge
incidence is declared to be a dimensionless auxiliary flag.
Lemma 6.37 (Carrier–activity hypergraph coverage and no-reuse allocation). For a connected family
(g, F) form the finite incidence hypergraph Hg,F whose ordinary edges are the physical carrier segments and
whose hyperedge A is incident to the selected pivots IP (A). Root the carrier skeleton by the deterministic
order of Lemma 6.34. Then:
    (i) cutting every carrier at U (F) ∪ Rg (F) leaves an acyclic carrier graph;
   (ii) the depth-first first-incidence rule assigns every activity hyperedge A exactly once, at the least
        incident pivot first reached from the root; its remaining n(A) − 1 incidences are recorded only as
        possible side branches;
  (iii) the three payments are disjoint: the product Fourier–Wiener norm pays the tensor-product nuclear
        cost on U (F), the scalar coverage reserve pays the lost clean-pivot contractions, and the promoted
        magnitude wA,k(A) is inserted exactly once in the rooted spatial recursion.
Consequently no same-edge or cross-edge activity, shared carrier tail, closure edge, or cycle-breaking pivot
is charged twice in (90) or in the recursion (94).
Proof. By definition, every carrier cycle meeting an activity is cut at all of that activity’s incident pivots,
and every remaining activity-free carrier cycle is cut at its least closure pivot in Rg (F). Hence a cycle in
the residual carrier graph would avoid both sets, contradicting (87); this proves (i). On the resulting
rooted forest, deterministic depth-first exploration gives a total order on first visits to pivots. Assign a
hyperedge at the first visited member of its incidence set. This assignment is unique, while every other
incidence can only attach a branch to an already assigned hyperedge, proving (ii). The product-group
convolution of Lemma 6.35 contains the complete activity factor and all covered one-pivot densities before
any scalar split. Lemma 6.31 then separates its nuclear loss from the unused scalar incidence reserve.
The first-incidence exploration retains one and only one copy of the promoted activity magnitude. These
operations act on distinct factors of the majorant, proving (iii) and the final claim.

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).

Here A G is the exact Gaussian cross-core inventory of Theorem 3.28, A reg and A irr are the exact
owner-map outputs, and A H is the nonvacuum compact inventory of Theorem 6.39. The scalar Zloc (J)
is formed only after every variable owned by a one-core reference has been integrated. Every physical,
normalization, seam, wrapping, determinant, full-incidence compact-vacuum, and source occurrence is
present exactly once in either a one-core reference or one of the four disjoint cross-core inventories; every
internally nontrivial compact singlet is in AJH .
Proof. Start from (53). Apply the exact Gaussian product identity of Theorem 3.28, the canonical owner
map, and Theorem 6.39. Convert each connected Gaussian or logarithmic cross-core word to its exact
Mayer factor e−GA − 1. Expand first over arbitrary finite subsets, including overlapping supports. Collect
each connected overlap component onto its least canonical reported hull; the resulting hull activities have
disjoint exact histories. Only after this overlap collect is performed are the remaining hull families declared
compatible. History compatibility therefore makes the four ownership classes disjoint and exhaustive
without omitting an overlapping Mayer term. Fubini gives (100); no scalar depending on an unintegrated
variable enters Zloc .

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).
Corollary 6.40 (Exact post-compact joint partition). Fix the compatible optical-core family after the
finite RG merge. There are normalized one-core references dµref
                                                              X,J and one exact cross-core activity
inventory
                                AJ = AJG ⊔ AJreg ⊔ AJirr ⊔ AJH
such that                                   Z O

                                        =           X,J (ζX )
                                                  dµref                             zA,J (ζA ).          (100)
                               Zloc (J)
                                              X                 F compatible A∈F

Here A G is the exact Gaussian cross-core inventory of Theorem 3.28, A reg and A irr are the exact
owner-map outputs, and A H is the nonvacuum compact inventory of Theorem 6.39. The scalar Zloc (J)
is formed only after every variable owned by a one-core reference has been integrated. Every physical,
normalization, seam, wrapping, determinant, full-incidence compact-vacuum, and source occurrence is
present exactly once in either a one-core reference or one of the four disjoint cross-core inventories; every
internally nontrivial compact singlet is in AJH .
Proof. Start from (53). Apply the exact Gaussian product identity of Theorem 3.28, the canonical owner
map, and Theorem 6.39. Convert each connected Gaussian or logarithmic cross-core word to its exact
Mayer factor e−GA − 1. Expand first over arbitrary finite subsets, including overlapping supports. Collect
each connected overlap component onto its least canonical reported hull; the resulting hull activities have
disjoint exact histories. Only after this overlap collect is performed are the remaining hull families declared
compatible. History compatibility therefore makes the four ownership classes disjoint and exhaustive
without omitting an overlapping Mayer term. Fubini gives (100); no scalar depending on an unintegrated
variable enters Zloc .

and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).

Theorem 6.43 (Joint Haar–optical two-root theorem). For the complete logarithm (54), every connected
zero-, one-, or two-source kernel joining two bounded matching-scale buffers A, B satisfies

                                 |Kq (A, B)| ≤ Cq e−mE dist(A,B) ,       q = 0, 1, 2,                       (104)

with exponentially weighted row and column sums uniform in volume, cutoff, exterior configuration, and
preceding weak depth.
Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected
logarithm to its still-live optical field. Its vertices are the compact-integrated activities of (99), the regular
and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

corollary.

                     Bm ≤ qsp Bm + Pm (B0 , . . . , Bm−1 ) + Cm,Q ΛN,m ,         m = 1, 2,

and (234) follows without changing the unmarked admission coefficient.
   Finally apply the summed replica–BKAR logarithm to the exact post-compact identity, keeping the
two roots and every completed physical g mark distinguished. The unmarked branch mass stays below
its strict threshold; marked objects are finite forcing vertices. The complete canonical reported hull, not
a selected connector, carries the remaining exponential weight. This proves (235); (236) is its immediate
corollary.

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).

irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported

Lemma 6.37 (Carrier–activity hypergraph coverage and no-reuse allocation). For a connected family
(g, F) form the finite incidence hypergraph Hg,F whose ordinary edges are the physical carrier segments and
whose hyperedge A is incident to the selected pivots IP (A). Root the carrier skeleton by the deterministic
order of Lemma 6.34. Then:
    (i) cutting every carrier at U (F) ∪ Rg (F) leaves an acyclic carrier graph;
   (ii) the depth-first first-incidence rule assigns every activity hyperedge A exactly once, at the least
        incident pivot first reached from the root; its remaining n(A) − 1 incidences are recorded only as
        possible side branches;
  (iii) the three payments are disjoint: the product Fourier–Wiener norm pays the tensor-product nuclear
        cost on U (F), the scalar coverage reserve pays the lost clean-pivot contractions, and the promoted
        magnitude wA,k(A) is inserted exactly once in the rooted spatial recursion.
Consequently no same-edge or cross-edge activity, shared carrier tail, closure edge, or cycle-breaking pivot
is charged twice in (90) or in the recursion (94).
Proof. By definition, every carrier cycle meeting an activity is cut at all of that activity’s incident pivots,
and every remaining activity-free carrier cycle is cut at its least closure pivot in Rg (F). Hence a cycle in
the residual carrier graph would avoid both sets, contradicting (87); this proves (i). On the resulting
rooted forest, deterministic depth-first exploration gives a total order on first visits to pivots. Assign a
hyperedge at the first visited member of its incidence set. This assignment is unique, while every other
incidence can only attach a branch to an already assigned hyperedge, proving (ii). The product-group
convolution of Lemma 6.35 contains the complete activity factor and all covered one-pivot densities before
any scalar split. Lemma 6.31 then separates its nuclear loss from the unused scalar incidence reserve.
The first-incidence exploration retains one and only one copy of the promoted activity magnitude. These
operations act on distinct factors of the majorant, proving (iii) and the final claim.

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).

Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected

and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by

Lemma 6.32 (Cutoff-uniform junction row). Every fixed bulk, toron, seam, reflection, or wrapping
junction has operator norm at most one constant J∗ < ∞, independent of the Peter–Weyl cutoff. At
every fixed passive mark order k ≤ 3 + Q, the corresponding coefficientwise constants J∗,k are finite and

coefficientwise at every allocated total mark order at most 3 + Q. Here CQ,0 = 1, while CQ,k < ∞ is a

                                                                   −1
Proof. On an oriented path, write the pivot increment as Xp = Yp,−    Yp,+ . The triangular change from
pivot increments to successive cumulative products has unit Haar Jacobian. The one-pivot density
                                             −1
therefore gives the convolution kernel ρp (Yp,− Yp,+ ). Every multipivot activity depends on the same
increments and hence multiplies the product-group convolution density hU,F itself; it is not used as a
Schur multiplier on an already-formed trace-class kernel. Reversed orientations are handled by inversion,
which is isometric in A.

   Lemma 6.28 identifies the trace norm of convolution on L2 (GU ) with the Fourier–Wiener norm and
supplies the Banach-algebra and lifting properties. Together with the Lemma 6.29 and the definition of
wA,k give the unmarked estimate. At order k, Leibniz marks at most k one-pivot densities or activities;
replacing those unmarked norms by their marked norms produces CQ,k , not an exponential-in-|U | loss. This
gives (86). This is the step that treats same-edge and cross-edge activities in one actual tensor-product
cut space.

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made
once and are independent of source order. Then, for every fixed Q < ∞, after exact compact integration
the complete nonvacuum compact coefficients satisfy (99), uniformly through three passive optical marks
and Q physical-source marks. The constants have the Cauchy growth of one common positive source
radius.
Proof. Expand the exact single-denominator amplitude once by Lemma 6.34 and split every activity
by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity
factors into the one product-group convolution operator of Lemma 6.35. Its Fourier–Wiener norm pays
the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.
Clean activity-free physical cycles are closed at the deterministic pivots in Rg (F). The strict unmarked
recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen
geometry. The marked triangular extension sums all carrier branches, physical cycles, shared tails, seams,
reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).
Corollary 6.40 (Exact post-compact joint partition). Fix the compatible optical-core family after the
finite RG merge. There are normalized one-core references dµref
                                                              X,J and one exact cross-core activity
inventory
                                AJ = AJG ⊔ AJreg ⊔ AJirr ⊔ AJH
such that                                   Z O
                                Z(J)                                X       Y
                                        =           X,J (ζX )
                                                  dµref                             zA,J (ζA ).          (100)
                               Zloc (J)
                                              X                 F compatible A∈F

Here A G is the exact Gaussian cross-core inventory of Theorem 3.28, A reg and A irr are the exact
owner-map outputs, and A H is the nonvacuum compact inventory of Theorem 6.39. The scalar Zloc (J)
is formed only after every variable owned by a one-core reference has been integrated. Every physical,
normalization, seam, wrapping, determinant, full-incidence compact-vacuum, and source occurrence is
present exactly once in either a one-core reference or one of the four disjoint cross-core inventories; every
internally nontrivial compact singlet is in AJH .
Proof. Start from (53). Apply the exact Gaussian product identity of Theorem 3.28, the canonical owner
map, and Theorem 6.39. Convert each connected Gaussian or logarithmic cross-core word to its exact
Mayer factor e−GA − 1. Expand first over arbitrary finite subsets, including overlapping supports. Collect
each connected overlap component onto its least canonical reported hull; the resulting hull activities have
disjoint exact histories. Only after this overlap collect is performed are the remaining hull families declared
compatible. History compatibility therefore makes the four ownership classes disjoint and exhaustive
without omitting an overlapping Mayer term. Fubini gives (100); no scalar depending on an unintegrated
variable enters Zloc .

Then the complete joint branch mass is at most 3δB /4, strictly inside the same admission ball.
Proof. The regular supplier gives η0 ≤ C(uω + ∥R⋆ (u)∥[3] ), the bad-core mass is exponentially small, and
both κG (RG ) and ηH (RG ) tend to zero as RG → ∞. Choose the parameters in the displayed order. This
is the same strict inequality consumed by the existing irregular and Gaussian forest theorems.
Lemma 6.42 (Cycle-safe compact spatial row). The complete compact activity estimate is exactly (99).
In particular every discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch is weighted before summation,
and ηH (RG ) ≤ Ce−mH RG for one mH > 0.
Proof. This is the final paragraph of Section 6.5.4; the residual edge-weight reserve pays root and anchor
multiplicity.

irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported

Proof. Start from the exact physical identity (100). Apply the summed replica–BKAR connected

and irregular activities, the exact Gaussian cross-core activities of (5), and the finite local source roots.
This vertex inventory is exhaustive before any path classification: Lemma 6.7 and Proposition 6.8 allocate
every original fine-link factor exactly once, and Corollary 6.40 retains every cross-core Gaussian, regular,
irregular, or compact activity in one disjoint inventory. The summed replica–BKAR theorem, the marked
Gaussian block-polymer theorem, and (103) sum every branch inside their common strict admission ball.
   In a connected forest joining A to B, take the unique tree path between the roots. Each object on
this path is either an optical covariance bond, a compact-integrated Haar activity, or a reported refer-
ence/regular/irregular, seam, or wrapping connector. Optical bonds pay the exponent of Corollary 4.14;
the complete compact objects pay the full-support estimate (99), whose proof already includes every
discovery edge, closure edge, cycle, and off-spine branch; all other reported connectors pay their registered
support exponent. The triangle inequality on the canonical reported hulls makes the total reported
length at least dist(A, B) − C. Weakening the minimum exponent once gives mE > 0 and (104). Mixed
retained/optical vertices are ordinary vertices of this same forest, so no joint composite channel is lost by
marginalization. Marks through order two promote finitely many vertices and change only Cq .

Theorem 6.39 (Exact incidence-split product-group nuclear compact collect). Choose R and a finite
pivot count N from the unmarked conditions (84), (95), and (96), with N ≥ 3. These choices are made

by (71). For each family, absorb all covered-pivot densities and all same-edge or cross-edge activity

the actual tensor-product nuclear cost. The scalar incidence reserve pays that cost and every lost Haar
contraction, while the promoted activity magnitude remains once for the canonical rooted exploration.

recursion fixes R and N once. At every finite marked order the same-order coefficient is still qsp < 1
and all new terms are lower-triangular forcing, so source order changes constants but not the chosen

reflections, wrapping edges, and activity hyperedges. The resulting majorant proves absolute convergence
of the family expansion and permits the cutoff and finite-subfamily limits. The final spatial allocation
gives (99).

Lemma 6.37 (Carrier–activity hypergraph coverage and no-reuse allocation). For a connected family
(g, F) form the finite incidence hypergraph Hg,F whose ordinary edges are the physical carrier segments and
whose hyperedge A is incident to the selected pivots IP (A). Root the carrier skeleton by the deterministic
order of Lemma 6.34. Then:
    (i) cutting every carrier at U (F) ∪ Rg (F) leaves an acyclic carrier graph;
   (ii) the depth-first first-incidence rule assigns every activity hyperedge A exactly once, at the least
        incident pivot first reached from the root; its remaining n(A) − 1 incidences are recorded only as
        possible side branches;
  (iii) the three payments are disjoint: the product Fourier–Wiener norm pays the tensor-product nuclear
        cost on U (F), the scalar coverage reserve pays the lost clean-pivot contractions, and the promoted
        magnitude wA,k(A) is inserted exactly once in the rooted spatial recursion.
Consequently no same-edge or cross-edge activity, shared carrier tail, closure edge, or cycle-breaking pivot
is charged twice in (90) or in the recursion (94).
Proof. By definition, every carrier cycle meeting an activity is cut at all of that activity’s incident pivots,
and every remaining activity-free carrier cycle is cut at its least closure pivot in Rg (F). Hence a cycle in
the residual carrier graph would avoid both sets, contradicting (87); this proves (i). On the resulting
rooted forest, deterministic depth-first exploration gives a total order on first visits to pivots. Assign a
hyperedge at the first visited member of its incidence set. This assignment is unique, while every other
incidence can only attach a branch to an already assigned hyperedge, proving (ii). The product-group
convolution of Lemma 6.35 contains the complete activity factor and all covered one-pivot densities before
any scalar split. Lemma 6.31 then separates its nuclear loss from the unused scalar incidence reserve.
The first-incidence exploration retains one and only one copy of the promoted activity magnitude. These
operations act on distinct factors of the majorant, proving (iii) and the final claim.
