Theorem 7.2 (Retained probe-root influence row). For every bounded centered probe in a fixed matching-
scale source buffer, the exact normalized response satisfies the zero-, one-, and two-source weighted row
and column bounds of (106).
Proof. Insert the probe as one of the two declared root vertices before applying Theorem 6.43. The

joint logarithm.
Theorem 7.3 (Exact local-source retention and Gate–E closure). Let F be a bounded local matching
observable and let B be any bounded registered exterior perturbation supported at distance at least r from
F . Through source order two,

                             DJq ⟨F ; B⟩c J=0 ≤ CF,B,q e−mE r ,     q = 0, 1, 2.                      (107)

The same estimate holds for a finite family of compact-boundary or optical chart perturbations whose
centered score rows are uniformly bounded in the permanent marked norm. This is the boundary-to-center
statement consumed by the source forest.
Proof. Use F and B as the two declared roots in Theorem 6.43. The owner inventory is exhaustive at the

of compact boundary data, differentiation along a unit-speed group geodesic inserts its centered local
score as the exterior root; for an admitted optical chart interpolation the affine derivative inserts the
corresponding registered optical score. The marked row is uniform in the exterior data allowed by the
exact regular/irregular owner decomposition. Integrating the finite interpolation gives the same estimate.
No claim for arbitrary uncontrolled boundary measures is needed downstream.
Corollary 7.4 (Uniform two-buffer connector rows). Let I1 , I2 be bounded source, displacement, stress,

7.1    Acoustic exclusion at the exact matching call site
Lemma 7.5 (No massless retained reference at matching). In the exact post-compact joint partition of
Corollary 6.40, the retained compact field has product normalized Haar reference and has already been
integrated coefficientwise before the optical connected logarithm is taken. The massless tangent Feynman
Gaussian used in the weak normal-chart construction is not a live matching reference. The only Gaussian
reference left live is the normalized optical one-core product, and every cross-core optical term is contained
in the explicit inventory AJG .
Proof. The exact compact collect of Theorem 6.39 and the post-compact identity of Corollary 6.40 restore
product Haar on the retained compact variables and place every nonempty retained dependence into the
compact, Gaussian, regular, or irregular activity inventories. The weak tangent Gaussian contributes
no undeclared covariance edge to this matching logarithm. By contrast, the optical one-core references
remain live, while their off-diagonal quadratic blocks and other cross-core terms occur explicitly in AJG
and retain the spatial payment used in Theorem 6.43.
Lemma 7.6 (Vacuum factorization after the complete owner map). Fix the optical variables and expand
one compact coefficient in Peter–Weyl sectors. Suppose every compact cut separating two declared root
buffers carries the trivial representation. Remove only the cross-core objects already registered in the
four disjoint inventories of Corollary 6.40. If no registered connector remains between the roots, the
residual coefficient factorizes into one-core expectations and empty-support scalars. Its contribution to the
normalized connected two-root logarithm is zero.
Proof. Triviality on every separating compact cut permits repeated Haar–Fubini separation. The exact
incidence-split collect retains every internally nontrivial singlet in AJH , while the owner map assigns every
other cross-core occurrence to exactly one of AJG , AJreg , AJirr , or AJH . After those registered objects are
removed, the residual history has no object joining distinct one-core components. It therefore factorizes.
Empty-support scalars are removed by the normalization, and products of separate one-root expectations
are removed by the connected logarithm.
Theorem 7.7 (Exact acoustic exclusion-or-capture). Consider any connected zero-, one-, or two-source
coefficient joining two bounded matching-scale root buffers in the exact joint logarithm. At least one object
on the unique root-to-root forest path belongs to one of the following classes:
   (i) a nonvacuum compact activity controlled by Theorem 6.39 and the spatial row (99);
  (ii) an optical covariance bond or an optical Gaussian cross-core activity in AJG ;
 (iii) a registered reference, regular, irregular, seam, reflection, wrapping, normalization, determinant, or
       source connector with its original support payment.
There is no fourth class consisting of a massless retained acoustic reference edge.
Proof. Assume that a connected coefficient contains no object of types (i)–(iii). Absence of (i) makes
every separating compact cut vacuum. Absence of (iii) leaves no registered physical connector after the
complete owner map. Lemma 7.6 then reduces the compact contribution to disconnected one-core factors
and scalars. Absence of (ii) also prevents the still-live optical field from joining the roots. The coefficient
is therefore disconnected, a contradictio
